Each gate carries either a `status_package` or an explicit `status_basis_legacy`, and a ratchet compares the working tree against `git show HEAD:` -- a status that moves without a package is refused, and a gate that has once had a package may never return to `null`. **Claims are deliberately out of scope** and the registry says so: 21 packages, 14 of them for sealed history, would mostly be fiction. **The Basis column returns, generated.** It renders a package's `implication` where one exists and the legacy line otherwise. Re-plumbing working evidence into a second format would be churn against the auditor's own instruction that the migration be net-deleting. |

| 2026-08-04 | D-073 commit 1: add the status-board generator and its checker. No surface changes yet -- the artifact only. | **The board is generated from the registries and verified by BYTE EQUALITY against a fresh render. Counts at `24cacf3`. No gate movement.** | `.agent-harness/scripts/build_status_board.py`; `.agent-harness/generated/STATUS_BOARD.md`; `.agent-harness/scripts/check_ssot_consistency.py`; `.agent-harness/tests/test_ssot_consistency.py`; `.agent-harness/context/MUTATION_MANIFEST.json` | **Verification is byte equality, not a stored hash.** `build_context_pack.py` stores a version and the validator compares it, which is right for a verbatim concatenation where the question is 'have the inputs changed since the build'. This is a PROJECTION, so the stronger and cheaper question is 'is this what the current inputs render to' -- which re-rendering answers directly, with nothing stored that can itself go stale. **Two properties of the renderer are load-bearing rather than stylistic:** it emits no `## ` heading and **no date or timestamp**. Headings decide which sections are live and dates drive the ledger's newest-row rule, so a generator able to write either could move its own authority; and a date would make the bytes a function of the clock, dirtying the tree on every no-op rebuild. **Four guards, each with a mutation entry, all four pinned as required:** the artifact must equal a fresh render, it must be git-tracked, it may carry exactly one block (two is how a false board is smuggled in beside a true one), and the row count must equal the registries -- the last being the mechanical replacement for the coverage floor, catching a dropped row without anyone having to notice a missing line. **Three of my own errors were caught by existing machinery during this commit, and are recorded because that is what having it is for.** The fixture corpus needed the board CARRIED rather than the check weakened to tolerate its absence -- the same call made when the validator fixture had to carry its sibling checkers. The tracking check had to be gated on `is_git_repo`, since tracking is a property of git repositories and not every synthetic corpus is one. And **the mutation gate's own scratch builder missed UNTRACKED files**: it used `git diff --name-only HEAD`, so a commit introducing a brand-new script left that script out of the scratch tree and thirteen mutations failed there for reasons unrelated to any guard. It now uses `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all`. The gate separately refused a moved anchor as an ERROR rather than skipping it, which is how the `is_git_repo` edit was caught. |

No gate movement.** | `.agent-harness/tests/test_mutation_gate.py`; `.agent-harness/context/MUTATION_MANIFEST.json`; `.agent-harness/scripts/check_canary_freshness.py`; `.agent-harness/tests/test_canary_freshness.py`; `.agent-harness/runs/run-20260804-f10-d070-canary-c12/`; `.agent-harness/runs/run-20260804-f10-d070-canary-c13/` | **F10-DESIGN-002 -- the gate counted a crash as a kill.** A replacement of `if status == expected or:` made the checker unimportable, every named fixture failed for that reason, and the battery recorded a successful kill of a guard it had never exercised. Two checks now separate the cases: the mutated file must COMPILE, and every named test must appear as `FAILED` in pytest's per-test report rather than the run merely exiting non-zero. The compile check alone is insufficient -- valid Python can still break an import or a fixture -- which is why both exist. Verified against the auditor's exact string. **F10-DESIGN-003 -- the manifest had no schema and no floor.** `schema_version: 999` plus an unknown top-level key was accepted silently, and deleting an entry simply reduced parametrisation with the suite still green. The manifest is now closed-schema, and `required_ids` pins the 14 guards that may never lose their mutation: those that can change canonical gate state, admit or substitute evidence, authorise a retirement, or bind provenance. Deleting a NON-required entry stays legal, because the declared coverage is partial and pretending otherwise would be the fig leaf the auditor warned about. **F10-DESIGN-001 (HIGH) -- provenance verified blobs but not history.** `git show` reads unreachable objects, so amended-away and rebased-away commits passed; and nothing tied the named commit to the attestation, so a commit predating it passed too. Two relations close all three: the declared commit must be an ancestor of HEAD, and it must be the commit that INTRODUCED the attestation. Introducing rather than last-modifying, because the pin itself edits the attestation, so last-modifying can never equal what the pin names. **And the guard immediately found the same defect in two more canaries.** C9 declared `0aa33cf` while introduced at `c63ab3c`; C10 declared `c63ab3c` while introduced at `17d0a06`. B22 diagnosed this exact structural cause -- `git rev-parse HEAD` at attestation-writing time is necessarily the PREVIOUS commit -- fixed the convention for C11, and **did not re-check the two canaries already written**. Their attested digests do match at the commits they name, so their substantive claims held and only the citation was wrong. They are retired by replacement rather than edited: C12 re-runs the 24-way race (24 dispatches, 14 blocked, 10 idempotent, **1 consumed row**) and C13 re-runs the two-assignment discriminator (**1 consumed row**, the second refused by the per-agent flock). Live canaries are now C11, C12, C13. **Weakening the guard to accommodate C9 and C10 was rejected outright** -- narrowing a guard to fit bad evidence is the B9-to-B14 pattern this project has already named as a way guards stop guarding. |
| 2026-08-04 | D-071: seal the trajectory lane on a RE-DERIVED measurement, and withdraw the robustness envelope inside the decision its own forward reference pointed at | **`G-F10-INDEPENDENT-FLRW=FAIL` stays, now closed on current measurement. Counts at `6e7fc1e`.** Re-deriving rather than quoting was not ceremony: the first two attempts were both wrong. | `docs/audit/BD622_D071_trajectory_closure_2026-08-04.md`; `.agent-harness/context/GATE_REGISTRY.json` (`fail_disposition`); `.agent-harness/context/FROZEN_DECISIONS.md`; `docs/harness/DECISION_LOG.md` | **Two derivation errors, recorded because the next reader will otherwise repeat them.** First, the evaluation counter RESTARTS in each phase, so a naive regex over the whole stdout mixes the base phase -- which COMPLETED, reaching N=6.2145 in 3,651 evaluations -- with the domain holdout that stalled, and yields a nonsense 0.07-year estimate. Second, the domain holdout is NOT monotone: it peaks at N=0.1813 at evaluation 751, drops by **-0.0184**, then creeps. A window spanning that drop yields no finite estimate at all; a window starting below the final value manufactures drift. **The defensible window is the post-drop creep**, the regime the integrator was actually in when the budget expired: 2.376238e-07 N/eval at 4.42062 s/eval, leaving 7.771399 in N -- ~3.27e7 further evaluations, **4.58 years**, an **~8,305x** miss against the frozen 3,938-evaluation projection, with a **4.40-4.78 year** envelope from log rounding alone. That independently reproduces the prior adjudication and the third-party auditor (~4.66 y, ~8,446x) inside the envelope, which is exactly what re-deriving is for. **What the trace establishes with no extrapolation at all:** the last new maximum was set at evaluation 751, and the following 10,300 evaluations consumed 45,545 s -- **70% of the entire wall budget** -- without setting another. **Uncertainty stated rather than implied:** the rounding envelope is not a total uncertainty; model uncertainty is unquantified because no step-size trace, rejection counter or error norm survives; and the -0.0184 drop is unexplained and unmodelled, so if it recurs the projection is optimistic.
